### 431. assistant (2026-06-22T17:09:08.291Z)

Pairing caught a real blocker before you hit it. Here's the verdict:

**✅ The deploy is genuinely live and healthy** (nothing to install — cloudflared's already there):
- **Tunnel is connected + routing real traffic** — 3 QUIC connections (bom11/blr02/bom09), ingress correct (`app.` + `api.` → `127.0.0.1:8000`), and the log even shows a real `api.khelsutra.guru/api/compile` request hitting the engine earlier today.
- Engine up 13h, local `/api/health` → 200; CF Access gate confirmed; `cf_access_aud` matches; marketing site untouched.

**❌ The one blocker — the Gemini key is invalid** (this is exactly the silent "0 rallies" trap):
```
GET …/v1beta/models?key=AQ.Ab8…  →  HTTP 401  UNAUTHENTICATED
"Expected OAuth 2 access token, login cookie or other valid authentication credential"
```
The stored value (`AQ.Ab…`, len 53) is **not a Gemini API key** — it looks like an OAuth access token (`gcloud`-style). A real Gemini API key starts with **`AIza`** (~39 chars). The engine calls Gemini the same way, so every job would finish `done` with **0 rallies** and the error would only land in the log. Good thing we checked before sharing.

### The fix (needs you — I can't mint a key)
1. Create a real key at **https://aistudio.google.com/apikey** (a fresh one; revoke the `AQ.` one). It'll start with `AIza`.
2. Then either:
   - **You set it** (cleanest — keeps the secret out of this chat):
     ```bash
     ssh <you>@168.144.126.176
     sudo sed -i 's#^GEMINI_API_KEY=.*#GEMINI_API_KEY=AIza...your-key...#' /etc/khelsutra/engine.env
     sudo systemctl restart khelsutra-engine
     ```
   - **or paste it here** and I'll set it + restart + re-run the validity check in one shot. ⚠ Caveat: pasting puts it in this transcript, so you'd want to rotate it again after the alpha — your call.

Once the key's in, I'll re-run the `models?key=` check (expect `200`) to confirm it authenticates, and then you do the one thing only you can: open **https://app.khelsutra.guru**, sign in with the email OTP, and run a short clip through upload → process → build → download. I'll watch the engine logs live on the droplet while you do it so we catch anything immediately.

Want to set the key (or paste it), and I'll verify + tail the logs for your first real reel?

### 475. assistant (2026-06-22T17:33:10.435Z)

All healthy now — public edge `302` (gate up), fast (0.07s), engine clean, allowlist persisted. **The 522 is resolved.**

## What was wrong + the fix
Your deploy is **single-origin** — the engine serves the **KhelSutra Studio SPA** at `/` *and* the API at `/api`, with both `app.` and `api.` routed through the one tunnel. But `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` only listed `api.` (it was copied from the runbook's *split*-topology value, where `app.` would've been on Pages). So the engine's DNS-rebinding Host-guard was **400-ing every `app.` request** — the SPA couldn't load post-login. The 522 itself was a transient origin-unreachable blip (during one of my restarts); the real wall was that 400. **Fixed:** added `app.khelsutra.guru` to the allowlist → restarted → `app.` now serves the SPA (200), persisted to `engine.env`.

## Your input question
- **Local upload → YES.** That's the alpha path: the SPA uploads your file (chunked) into the jail, then processes. Use this.
- **`gs://` link → NO** on this box. With the `allowed_video_root` jail on (tenant isolation), `/api/process` rejects non-local URIs by design (400) — so a bucket URI won't work through the gated SPA.
- **Google Drive `https://` share link → NO.** The engine doesn't fetch arbitrary web URLs; supported schemes are local paths (uploads), `gs://`/`s3://`, and `gdrive://` (mount). A Drive web link isn't one. (The new `gdrive-api://` backend I just built is the future for this, but it's not wired into the hosted input yet + needs the OAuth app.)

## Go ahead — I'm watching the logs
**Hard-refresh** https://app.khelsutra.guru (Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+R to clear the cached 522) → CF Access login (OTP to your email) → the Studio SPA loads → **upload a short clip** → Process → pick → Build → download.

Tell me when you've kicked off Process (or just say "go") and I'll pull the live engine logs to watch the Gemini chunks + rally count come through in real time. (Heads-up: a *long* match can still hit a 524 on the Build step — that's the known async-compile gap, engine #329; a short clip avoids it.)

Also worth a quick follow-up later (not blocking you): the deploy docs (`deploy/droplet/engine.env.example` + `DEPLOY_ALPHA.md` §2.2) still carry the `api.`-only allowlist, which is wrong for this single-origin setup — I can PR a fix so the next provision doesn't hit this.

### 485. assistant (2026-06-22T17:41:42.702Z)

Post-auth 522 (after SSO) but `api.` works — that's the tell. A **522 means Cloudflare tried to reach the origin and the TCP connection timed out**, which in a tunnel setup means the request *isn't going through the tunnel at all*. Most likely: `api.`'s DNS is the tunnel CNAME (works), but **`app.`'s DNS record isn't routed to the tunnel** (it's pointing at the droplet's public IP / a stale record), so CF tries to hit the box directly on 443 → the engine's only on loopback → timeout → 522.

The tunnel *ingress* has `app.` (I saw it), but the **DNS route** for `app.` is the missing piece. Let me verify and fix it:

### 489. assistant (2026-06-22T17:43:29.049Z)

Root cause pinned. The tunnel is remotely-managed, **tunnel ID `55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac`**. `api.` works because its DNS routes into the tunnel; **`app.`'s DNS record is *not* pointed at the tunnel** (it's resolving to the box/origin directly), so Cloudflare tries to reach the droplet on :443 — where nothing's listening (the engine is loopback-only) — → **522**. The tunnel's *ingress* has `app.` (I saw it), but the *DNS record* is the missing/wrong half. This is a Cloudflare-side fix I can't make from the box (no zone-DNS credential there).

## The fix (CF dashboard — ~1 min)

**Cloudflare dashboard → `khelsutra.guru` zone → DNS → Records.** Look at the `app` record and compare it to `api`:
- `api` is a **CNAME → `55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac.cfargotunnel.com`** (Proxied / orange cloud). That's why it works.
- `app` is almost certainly something else (a proxied **A** record to the droplet IP, or a stale record). 

**Make `app` match `api`:** edit/replace the `app` record to be a **CNAME**, target **`55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac.cfargotunnel.com`**, **Proxied (orange cloud ON)**. If Cloudflare won't let you (a conflicting record exists), **delete the old `app` record first**, then add the CNAME.

*(Equivalent path: Zero Trust → Networks → Tunnels → your tunnel → Public Hostnames — confirm `app.khelsutra.guru` is listed pointing at `http://127.0.0.1:8000`; if a conflicting DNS record is flagged, removing it lets the tunnel manage `app`'s DNS automatically.)*

Once saved, give it ~30s and reload `app.khelsutra.guru` (the Edge window) — it'll route through the tunnel to the engine and the SPA will load.

- `api.khelsutra.guru` = **Tunnel → khelsutra-studio** → reaches the engine. Works.
- `app.khelsutra.guru` = **CNAME → khelsutra.pages.dev** → points at a **Cloudflare Pages** project that isn't serving (→ the 522 after login). The engine — which *already* serves a working SPA — never sees the `app.` request.

So if `app.` routes to the tunnel/engine, the SPA loads *and* its API calls hit the same engine — **no CORS, no preflight, nothing else to configure.** The engine already has `app.` in its allowlist (I set that) + a valid key. So this one DNS change makes the whole flow work.

## The exact change to make — repoint `app.` to the tunnel (same as `api.`)

**Simplest (one edit):** DNS → click **Edit** on the `app.khelsutra.guru` row → change the record from

- ✅ `CNAME` → **`55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac.cfargotunnel.com`**  (keep **Proxied / orange cloud ON**)

Either way you end up with `app` and `api` both pointing at `khelsutra-studio`.

**Why this over fixing the Pages project:** Pages would be the "split" design (SPA on CDN), but that project is broken, and it'd then need a cross-origin API base + the CORS OPTIONS-bypass. ### 529. assistant (2026-06-22T18:13:45.435Z)

Grounded — and the owner's instinct is right on both counts. Current state (verified against `origin/master`):
- **Backend is already refresh-safe** ✅ — jobs run server-side, persisted in the SQLite `jobs` table; a client refresh doesn't touch the running job.
- **But the SPA loses the in-flight `job_id` on refresh** — `useIngestJob.ts` holds it only in poll-loop state (no `localStorage`/URL persistence), so a refresh drops you back to the ingest form even though the job keeps running.
- **And progress looks static** — the SPA reflects `job.progress` (a coarse free-text phase), but the Gemini segmenter never pushes per-chunk progress (it *logs* "Extracting Chunk 3/7" but doesn't call the job-progress hook), so the text doesn't move for minutes.

Let me check for an existing issue, then file it:

### 535. assistant (2026-06-22T18:16:24.112Z)

Filed the feature request — and here's the cost answer.

## ✅ Feature request filed
[avidullu/khelsutra#111](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/issues/111) — "Resilient processing UX: survive page refresh + continually-updating progress" (`enhancement`). It captures both halves accurately: backend is *already* refresh-safe (jobs are DB-durable), so the work is (a) persist+resume the `job_id` client-side across refresh, (b) a heartbeat/elapsed progress indicator, and (c) a linked engine tweak to push per-chunk progress.

## 💰 Cost for this run (estimated — the engine doesn't meter it yet)
Verified facts: the engine uses `gemini-flash-latest` at **default media resolution** (it doesn't set `media_resolution=low`), and this run sent **7 chunks × 90s ≈ 630s of video** to Gemini.

- Video input ≈ 630s × ~300 tokens/s ≈ **~190k input tokens**; output (rally JSON) is negligible.
- `gemini-flash-latest` currently resolves to a **Gemini 3 Flash** build (~$0.50/1M input, per the official changelog — high confidence): **≈ $0.10 for this ~8-min match** (~**$0.01 per minute of footage**). *(If the alias has since moved to the pricier 3.5-Flash GA at $1.50/1M, it'd be ~$0.30 — check AI Studio billing for the truth.)*
- **OR $0 if your key is on Gemini's free tier** — but ⚠ **free-tier traffic can be used by Google to train on**, whereas the **paid tier does not train on your data**. For an alpha handling users' footage, you likely want billing enabled (paid) for the privacy guarantee — it's only pennies. Check AI Studio → your key's project to see which tier you're on.

Fixed costs (droplet ~$24/mo, Cloudflare $0) are not per-run, so **per-run ≈ the Gemini cost above**.

**Two follow-ups worth noting:**
1. The **M8 cost-ledger is built to show this per-job in the UI**, but the Gemini `usageMetadata` (token counts) isn't wired into it yet (the documented M8 gap). Wiring that = exact per-run cost, automatically. Want me to file that issue?
2. Setting `media_resolution=low` (~100 tok/s) would **cut the Gemini cost ~3×** (~$0.03/match) for some accuracy tradeoff — a cheap config knob.

## ⏳ Your job
Still running (~10 min in) — no errors, poll loop healthy. The slowness is the CPU droplet chewing through **60fps 1080p** frame extraction across 7 chunks (the smoke clip was 30fps/3 chunks and finished in ~2 min). This is exactly why your **small backup clip** is the better demo of the full upload→reel→download, and why the per-match speed argues for the GPU/Cloud-Run path later. I'll report the rally count the moment it lands.

### 558. assistant (2026-06-22T18:26:32.860Z)

Checkpoint saved to memory (tonight's milestone + the two fixes + the two bugs + the next-phase plan). The priced comparison is researching now — but here's the **decision framing** so you can sleep on it; the numbers will fill in shortly:

**The shape of the call (independent of exact prices):**
- **Your workload is small.** WASB/TrackNet is a *tiny* CV model (well under 8 GB VRAM), and the owned-model corpus is ~6–15 matches. So you do **not** need an A100/H100 — that's the expensive trap. The real choice is **T4 (cheapest "real" GPU) vs L4 (already proven on the M7 run, better perf/$)**. I'll price both.
- **Two cost levers that matter more than the GPU choice:** (1) **Spot/preemptible** instances (~60–80% off — fine for batch testing/training where an interruption just means re-run), and (2) **spin-up → work → DELETE** (you only pay while it's up; an idle GPU VM is the money sink). With those, this phase is pennies-to-a-few-dollars, not a monthly bill.
- **Form factor:** a **GPU VM you SSH into** (via the existing `deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh` — auto Ops-Agent, zone-sweep) for interactive train/infer/harness work — *not* Cloud Run (that's for serving). Pull the corpus straight from `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus` so there's no local-box dependency.
- **GCP vs DO:** your ADR-013 already picked GCP (DO GPU isn't even enabled on your account), but I'm pulling DO/Paperspace numbers too so you can confirm the call with data rather than take it on faith.

So my recommendation will almost certainly be: **GCP L4 (or T4 if you want rock-bottom) on Spot, in Singapore (Mumbai L4 stocks out), spun up per-session and deleted after** — and the harness is already there to point at the bucket. I'll come back with the priced table (T4/L4/A100 × on-demand/Spot × region, + DO/Paperspace) and a concrete "tomorrow morning" runbook the moment the research lands. Get some sleep — this is a great place to pause. 🏸

### 564. assistant (2026-06-22T18:28:12.435Z)

Filed: [avidull/khelsutra#112](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/issues/112) (`bug`) — "Share to WhatsApp sends only a text message, not the reel video." Root cause is the hardcoded `wa.me/?text=` link (text-only by design); the fix is the **Web Share API with the file** (`navigator.share({ files: [reelBlob] })`) so the actual video goes to WhatsApp on capable browsers, with a graceful download-then-attach fallback on desktop. (Honest note in the issue: Edge/desktop may still fall back — the one-tap win lands on mobile, which is where reels get shared anyway.)

**Your first live alpha run surfaced a tidy hardening backlog** — exactly what a real run should do:
| # | What | Repo |
|---|---|---|
| [#111](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/issues/111) | Survive refresh + live progress (don't time out / re-trigger) | khelsutra |
| [#112](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/issues/112) | WhatsApp share the **video**, not just text | khelsutra |
| [#340](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/340) | `/api/process` idempotent — no Gemini re-bill on refresh | engine |

All three are Claude-doable, default-safe — natural pickups for the next session (they're the "make the alpha robust + cheap + shareable" trio).

The **GPU-config comparison is still researching** — I'll drop the priced table (T4/L4/A100 × on-demand/Spot × region, + DO/Paperspace) and the "tomorrow morning" runbook the moment it lands. Genuinely great night's work: a real reel out of the cloud alpha, three sharp bugs captured from live use, and the next phase teed up. 🏸

### 579. user (2026-06-22T18:34:27.259Z)

yeah! Last question, how do I add someone to the CF Access policy?

### 580. assistant (2026-06-22T18:34:57.494Z)

Quick one — it's a Cloudflare **Zero Trust** dashboard edit (not the DNS page):

1. **[one.dash.cloudflare.com](https://one.dash.cloudflare.com)** → **Access → Applications**
2. Open the application that covers **`app.khelsutra.guru`** (the same one gates `api.` too — one app, so one edit covers both) → **Edit**
3. **Policies** tab → open your existing **Allow** policy (the one with the *Emails* rule that currently has `avi.dullu@gmail.com`)
4. In the **Include → Emails** rule, **add the tester's email**(s) → **Save**

That's it — effective on their next visit.

**What the tester does:** goes to `app.khelsutra.guru` → enters their email → gets a **one-time PIN** in their inbox → enters it → they're in (no Cloudflare account needed; session lasts hours). GitHub SSO also works if they prefer, since that IdP's wired up.

**Two tips:**
- **For more than a handful of testers**, use **Access → Lists** (or "Emails ending in `@yourdomain`") instead of editing the policy each time — define the list once, reference it in the rule, then just add emails to the list. Cleaner at scale.
- **It's free up to 50 users**, so the whole alpha cohort costs nothing on the gate.

(I'd offer to add someone via the API, but the token you dropped is read-only — DNS-scoped — and Access-policy edits need an `Access: Edit` scope anyway, so the dashboard is genuinely the fastest path here.)

That's the last piece — your alpha's ready to share. 🏸
